[Previous
Version](section-60.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 60.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Appeal to Canadian International Trade Tribunal]{.HTitleText3} {#h-438568 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to Tribunal

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 77.012 or 77.12,
  a person who deems himself aggrieved by a re-determination of the
  President made pursuant to section 59 with respect to any goods may
  appeal therefrom to the Tribunal by filing a notice of appeal in
  writing with the President and the Tribunal within ninety days after
  the day on which the re-determination was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal --- scope ruling

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Interested persons may appeal a scope ruling made
  under section 66 or, an amendment to such a ruling resulting from a
  review under subsection 67(2) or, subject to section 77.012 or 77.12
  and only in respect of whether goods are of the same description as
  goods described in the order or finding, a re-determination made under
  paragraph 59(1)(a) or (e), to the Tribunal by filing a notice of
  appeal in writing with the President and the Tribunal within 90 days
  after the day on which the decision was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of notice of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of the hearing of an appeal under
  subsection (1) or (1.1) must be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) at least 21 days before the day of
  the hearing, and any person who enters an appearance with the Tribunal
  at least seven days before the day of the hearing may be heard on the
  appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order or finding of the Tribunal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On any appeal under subsection (1) or (1.1), the
  Tribunal may make such order or finding as the nature of the matter
  may require and, without limiting the generality of the foregoing, may
  declare what duty is payable or that no duty is payable on the goods
  with respect to which the appeal was taken, and an order, finding or
  declaration of the Tribunal is final and conclusive subject to further
  appeal as provided in section 62.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 61
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 1993, c. 44, s. 216
- 1999, c. 12, s. 35, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
- 2014, c. 20, ss. 436, 443
- 2017, c. 20, s. 87
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-61-20141101.html "Link to previous version of section 61"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Appeal to Federal Court]{.HTitleText3} {#h-438580 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to Federal Court on question of
law

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any of
  the parties to an appeal under section 61, namely,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person who appealed,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the President, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any person who entered an appearance in accordance
    with subsection 61(2),

  may, within ninety days after the making of an order or finding under
  subsection 61(3), appeal therefrom to the Federal Court of Appeal on
  any question of law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Federal Court of Appeal may dispose of an appeal
  by making such order or finding as the nature of the matter may
  require and, without limiting the generality of the foregoing, may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} declare what duty is payable or that no duty is
    payable on the goods with respect to which the appeal to the
    Tribunal was taken; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} refer the matter back to the Tribunal for
    re-hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 62
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 1990, c. 8, s. 72
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
- 2017, c. 20, s. 88
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-62-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 62"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[62.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62.1 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c.
25, s. 98\]]{.Repealed}

### [Scope Ruling]{.HTitleText2} {#h-438595 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  interested person may submit an application to the President for a
  scope ruling with respect to any goods.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application review period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The President shall, within 30 days after the day on
  which he or she receives the application, determine if it should be
  rejected or if a scope proceeding should be initiated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of review period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The President may extend the period set out in
  subsection (2) to 45 days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescribed criteria

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The President shall reject the application if any
  criteria prescribing the rejection of an application apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescribed circumstances

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The President may reject an application in the
  prescribed circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The President shall provide written notice to the
  applicant if their application is rejected under subsection (4) or (5)
  and give reasons for the rejection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incomplete application

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If an application is incomplete, the notice referred
  to in subsection (6) must identify the deficiencies in the
  application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initiation of scope proceeding

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If the President does not reject the application
  under subsection (4) or (5), the President shall initiate a scope
  proceeding with respect to the goods that are the subject of the
  application.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 63
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}President's initiative

**[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** The President may
initiate a scope proceeding with respect to any goods at any time, on
the President's own initiative.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 64
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of scope proceeding

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** If a scope proceeding is
initiated under subsection 63(8) or section 64, the President shall
provide written notice to the applicant, if any, the government of the
country of export, the exporter, the importer and the domestic
producers.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 65
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope ruling

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  on or before the one hundred twentieth day after the initiation of a
  scope proceeding under subsection 63(8) or section 64, the President
  shall make a scope ruling and provide reasons for it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The President may, in the prescribed circumstances,
  extend the period set out in subsection (1) to 210 days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At any time before the President makes a scope ruling
  under subsection (1), the President may cause the scope proceeding to
  be terminated in the prescribed circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope ruling --- effective date

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A scope ruling made under subsection (1) takes effect
  on the day on which it is made, unless the President indicates
  otherwise, and it includes any terms and conditions that the President
  considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The President shall give written notice to the
  government of the country of export and the applicant, if any, of:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an extension given under subsection (2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the making of a scope ruling under subsection (1);
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the termination of a scope proceeding under
    subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors for ruling

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In making a scope ruling, the President shall take
  into account any prescribed factors and any other factor that the
  President considers relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ruling final

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A scope ruling made under subsection (1) is final and
  conclusive, subject to further appeal as provided in subsection
  61(1.1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 66
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of ruling

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of giving effect to a decision of the Tribunal, the
  Federal Court of Appeal or the Supreme Court of Canada, the President
  shall review the scope ruling made under subsection 66(1) to which
  that Tribunal or Court decision relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review --- prescribed circumstances

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The President may review a scope ruling made under
  subsection 66(1) under prescribed circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confirmation, amendment or revocation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The President shall confirm, amend or revoke a scope
  ruling reviewed under subsection (1) or (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The President shall give written notice to the
  government of the country of export, the applicant, if any, and
  interested persons of a review undertaken under subsection (1) or (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 67
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of scope ruling

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** A scope ruling applies to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any determination or re-determination under sections
  55, 56 and 57 and paragraphs 59(1)(a) and (e); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any decision relating to whether an undertaking in
  respect of which an investigation has been suspended under
  subparagraph 50(a)(iii) applies to goods.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 68
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Binding decision

**[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** Subject to the
regulations, a scope ruling is binding with respect to any decision,
determination or re-determination made by a designated officer or the
President in respect of any goods to which that scope ruling applies
that are released on or after its effective date.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 69
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application --- section 55

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  scope ruling may be applied by the President to any determination made
  under section 55 in respect of any goods which are accounted for under
  subsection 32(1), (3) or (5) of the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6),
  no more than two years before the effective date of the scope ruling
  if, no later than 90 days after that effective date,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the importer of the goods

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} makes a written request in the prescribed form
      and manner and accompanied by the prescribed information, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has paid all duties owing on the goods; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the goods are goods of a CUSMA country, the
    government of that CUSMA country or, if they are of that CUSMA
    country, the producer, manufacturer or exporter of the goods makes a
    request as described in subparagraph (a)(i), whether or not the
    importer of the goods has paid all duties owing on the goods.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application --- section 56

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A scope ruling may be applied by a designated officer
  to any determination made under section 56 no more than two years
  before the ruling's effective date if the determination has not
  previously been re-determined under section 57 or 59 and, no later
  than 90 days after that date,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the importer of the goods

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} makes a written request in the prescribed form
      and manner and accompanied by the prescribed information, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has paid all duties owing on the goods; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the goods are goods of a CUSMA country, the
    government of that CUSMA country or, if they are of that CUSMA
    country, the producer, manufacturer or exporter of the goods makes a
    request as described in subparagraph (a)(i), whether or not the
    importer of the goods has paid all duties owing on the goods.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application --- section 57 or 59

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A scope ruling may be applied by the President to any
  re-determination made under section 57 or paragraph 59(1)(a) or (e) in
  respect of any goods that are the subject of a determination made
  under subsection 56(1) or (2) no more than two years before the
  ruling's effective date if, no later than 90 days after that date,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the importer of the goods

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} makes a written request in the prescribed form
      and manner and accompanied by the prescribed information, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has paid all duties owing on the goods; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the goods are goods of a CUSMA country, the
    government of that CUSMA country or, if they are of that CUSMA
    country, the producer, manufacturer or exporter of the goods makes a
    request as described in subparagraph (a)(i), whether or not the
    importer of the goods has paid all duties owing on the goods.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope ruling --- designated officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A scope ruling may be applied by a designated officer
  to any determination made under section 56 no more than two years
  before the ruling's effective date if the designated officer deems it
  advisable within two years after the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope ruling --- President

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A scope ruling may be applied by the President to any
  determination made under section 55 or any re-determination made under
  section 56 or 57 or paragraph 59(1)(a) or (e) no more than two years
  before the ruling's effective date if the President deems it advisable
  within two years after the day on which the determination or
  re-determination is made, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requests under subsection (1) or (3)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a request made under subsection (1) or
  (3), the President shall make a decision within one year after the day
  on which the request was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of decision

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Except for the purposes of making a request under
  subsection 58(1.1) or section 77.011 or filing a notice of appeal
  under subsection 61(1), a decision made under subsection (4) or (5)
  within 90 days after the scope ruling's effective date is deemed to be
  made on that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed decision --- subsection (1), (3)
  or (5)

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A decision made under subsection (1), (3) or (5) is
  deemed to be a re-determination made by the President under subsection
  59(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed decision --- subsection (2) or
  (4)

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A decision made under subsection (2) or (4) is deemed
  to be a re-determination made by a designated officer under section
  57.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 70
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
- [2020, c. 1, s. 79](#1237774-1253739){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237774-1253739.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-70-20180426.html "Link to previous version of section 70"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Anti-circumvention Investigations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-438697 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[circumvention]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 72 to 75.6, [[circumvention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
situation in which all of the following exist:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a change has occurred in the pattern of trade since
  the day on which an order imposing a countervailing duty was made
  under section 7 or an investigation was initiated under section 31, as
  the case may be;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a prescribed activity is occurring and imports of the
  goods to which that prescribed activity applies are undermining the
  remedial effects of the order in council or the order or finding of
  the Tribunal; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the change in trade pattern is caused by the
  imposition of anti-dumping or countervailing duties.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 71
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
- [2022, c. 10, s. 198](#1357078-1367862){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357078-1367862.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-71-20180426.html "Link to previous version of section 71"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initiation of investigation

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  President shall cause an investigation to be initiated respecting the
  circumvention of an order or finding of the Tribunal, or an order of
  the Governor in Council imposing a countervailing duty under section
  7, on the President's own initiative or, if he or she receives a
  written complaint respecting the circumvention, within 45 days after
  the day on which that complaint is received, if he or she is of the
  opinion that there is evidence disclosing a reasonable indication that
  circumvention is occurring.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation --- exporter or country

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An anti-circumvention investigation may be initiated
  in respect of an exporter or in respect of a country, as the
  circumstances require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint --- required information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A complaint made under subsection (1) shall contain
  the information supporting the allegations that is reasonably
  available to the complainant and any other prescribed information.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 72
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 208, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
- [2022, c. 10, s. 199](#1357080-1367864){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357080-1367864.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-72-20180426.html "Link to previous version of section 72"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of investigation

- **[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  President causes an anti-circumvention investigation to be initiated,
  he or she shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} cause notice of the investigation to be

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} given to the importer, the exporter, the
      government of the exporting country, the domestic producers and
      the complainant, if any, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} published in the [Canada
      Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} publish the reasons for initiating the
    investigation in the prescribed manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}President decides not to initiate
  investigation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, after receipt of a complaint referred to in
  subsection 72(1), the President decides, with respect to some or all
  of the goods specified in the complaint, not to cause an investigation
  to be initiated, the President shall send a written notice of the
  decision and the reasons for it to the complainant.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 73
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of essential facts

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  President shall publish, in the prescribed manner, a statement of
  essential facts in respect of an investigation initiated under
  subsection 72(1) that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the President's preliminary assessment of whether
    the evidence discloses a reasonable indication of circumvention; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the facts the President relied on in
    making that preliminary assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before making a decision under subsection 75.1(1),
  the President shall allow interested parties sufficient time to
  provide written comments on the statement of essential facts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of statement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The President shall give notice of the publication of
  the statement of essential facts to the importer, the exporter, the
  government of the exporting country, the domestic producers and the
  complainant, if any.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 74
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

- **[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 74, the President
  may terminate an investigation at any time before the publication of
  the statement of essential facts, if the President is satisfied that
  the goods in respect of which an investigation was initiated under
  subsection 72(1) are of the same description as goods to which an
  order or finding of the Tribunal or an order of the Governor in
  Council applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In making a decision under subsection (1), the
  President shall take into account the factors referred to in
  subsection 66(6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of termination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an investigation is terminated under subsection
  (1), the President shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give notice of the termination to the importer, the
    exporter, the government of the exporting country, the domestic
    producers and the complainant, if any;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} publish notice of the termination in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} on the same day that the notice is given, publish
    the reasons for terminating the investigation in the prescribed
    manner, including the reasons for determining that the goods in
    question are of the same description as goods to which an order or
    finding of the Tribunal or an order of the Governor in Council
    applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope ruling

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A decision made under subsection (1) is deemed to be
  a scope ruling made under subsection 66(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 75
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision --- circumvention

- **[[75.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 75(1), within
  180 days after initiating an investigation under subsection 72(1), the
  President shall make a decision and shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} cause written notice of the decision to be

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} given to the importer, the exporter, the
      government of the exporting country, the domestic producers and
      the complainant, if any, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} published in the [Canada
      Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} publish the reasons for the decision in the
    prescribed manner; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the President finding that there is
    circumvention, file with the Tribunal the decision, the reasons for
    the decision and any other material relating to the decision that
    may be required under the rules of the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumvention

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In making the decision under subsection (1), the
  President may only make a finding of circumvention if, on the
  available evidence, the President is satisfied that importation of
  some or all of the goods in question constitutes circumvention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Details of decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A decision setting out a finding of circumvention
  must specify

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the goods to which it applies; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the exporters and the exporting countries to which
    it applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms and conditions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A decision setting out a finding of circumvention may
  include any terms and conditions that the President considers
  appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time period

- **[[75.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The President may, at any time
  before the publication of the statement of essential facts and before
  the expiry of the 180-day period set out in subsection 75.1(1), extend
  the period set out in that subsection to 240 days, in prescribed
  circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of extension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The President shall cause written notice of any
  extension to be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} given to the importer, the exporter, the government
    of the exporting country, the domestic producers and the
    complainant, if any; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} published in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tribunal

**[[75.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75.3 .sectionLabel}** Without delay after a
decision setting out a finding of circumvention is filed with the
Tribunal under paragraph 75.1(1)(c), the Tribunal shall make an order
amending the order or finding that is the subject of the President's
decision in the manner described in the decision, including any terms
and conditions that are set out in the decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim review

- **[[75.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The President may, on his or her own
  initiative or at the request of the Minister of Finance or any other
  person, the Tribunal or a government, conduct an interim review of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a decision made under subsection 75.1(1) that sets
    out a finding of circumvention; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any aspect of a decision referred to in paragraph
    (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Included decisions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), a decision made
  under subsection 75.1(1) is deemed to include any decision relating to
  it that is made under subsection (6) or 75.6(5) prior to the
  initiation of a review under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The President shall not conduct an interim review at
  the request of any person or government or the Tribunal unless the
  person or government or the Tribunal satisfies the President that the
  review is warranted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision if interim review not
  initiated

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the President decides not to conduct an interim
  review at the request of a person or government, the President shall
  forward a copy of the decision and the reasons for it to that person
  or government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initiation of interim review

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the President decides to conduct an interim review
  the President shall cause written notice of the decision to be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} given to the importer, the exporter, the government
    of the exporting country, the domestic producers, and the person or
    government requesting the review, if any; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} published in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} On completion of an interim review, the President
  shall make a decision rescinding the decision under review or
  confirming it, with or without amendment, as the circumstances
  require, and shall give reasons for making the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On completion of an interim review, the President
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} cause written notice of the decision made under
    subsection (6) to be given to the importer, the exporter, the
    government of the exporting country, the domestic producers and the
    person or government requesting the review, if any;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} cause the notice to be published in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} publish the reasons for that decision in the
    prescribed manner; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the decision requires the amendment of an order
    or finding of the Tribunal, file with the Tribunal the decision, the
    reasons for the decision and any other material relating to the
    decision that may be required under the rules of the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tribunal

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Without delay after a decision is filed with the
  Tribunal under paragraph (7)(d), the Tribunal shall make an order
  amending the order or finding that is the subject of the President's
  decision in the manner described in the decision, including any terms
  and conditions that are set out in the decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of decision

- **[[75.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of giving effect to
  a decision of the Federal Court of Appeal or the Supreme Court of
  Canada, the President shall review the decision or determination made
  under subsection 75.1(1), 75.4(6) or 75.6(5) to which that Court
  decision relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confirmation, amendment or revocation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The President shall confirm, amend or revoke a
  decision reviewed under subsection (1) and the confirmation, amendment
  or revocation is deemed, except for the purposes of section 96.1, to
  be a decision or determination made under subsection 75.1(1), 75.4(6)
  or 75.6(5), as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for exemption

- **[[75.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75.6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  exporter to Canada of any goods to which an order or finding referred
  to in subsection 3(1.1) or (1.2) applies may request that the
  President determine whether the goods from that exporter may be exempt
  from the extension of duties if the exporter

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establishes that they are not associated with any
    exporter who was given notice of the circumvention investigation;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has not been

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} given notice of the initiation of that
      investigation, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} requested to provide information during the
      course of that investigation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of request

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A request under subsection (1) shall be made in the
  prescribed manner and form and shall contain the prescribed
  information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review --- circumvention

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the President receives a request under
  subsection (1) and he or she is satisfied that a review is warranted,
  the President shall initiate a review, on an expedited basis, in order
  to determine whether goods from that exporter may be exempt from the
  extension of duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a review is initiated under subsection (3), the
  President shall provide written notice to the importer, the exporter,
  the government of the exporting country and the domestic producers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Upon completion of the review, the President shall
  make a determination that the goods from the exporter are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to the extension of duties if the President
    is satisfied that circumvention is occurring; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exempt from the extension of duties if the
    President is satisfied that no circumvention is occurring.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Upon completion of the review, the President shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} cause notice of the determination to be given to
    the importer, the exporter, the government of the exporting country,
    the domestic producers and the person requesting the review; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the President makes a determination under
    paragraph (5)(b), cause to be filed with the Tribunal:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} written notice of the determination, stating the
      reasons for the determination, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any other material that may be required under
      the rules of the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment --- order or finding

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Without delay after receipt of a notice under
  paragraph (6)(b), the Tribunal shall make an order amending the order
  or finding that is affected by the review in order to give effect to
  the President's determination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of investigation or review

- **[[75.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The President may terminate any
  investigation initiated under subsection 72(1) or any review initiated
  under subsection 75.4(1) or 75.6(3) in respect of any goods of an
  exporter or country, if the order or finding of the Tribunal or the
  order of the Governor in Council in respect of which the investigation
  or review is undertaken has expired or been rescinded or amended in
  respect of those goods prior to the conclusion of the investigation or
  review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of termination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The President shall cause written notice of the
  termination of an investigation or review under subsection (1) to be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} given to the importer, the exporter, the government
    of the exporting country, the domestic producers and, if
    appropriate, to the complainant or applicant, if any; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} published in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) if the termination is in
    relation to an investigation initiated under subsection 72(1) or a
    review initiated under subsection 75.4(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 89
:::

#### [Review of Orders and Findings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-438851 .Subheading}

##### [Judicial Review]{.HTitleText4} {#h-438852 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for judicial review

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** Subject to subsection
61(3) and Part I.1 or II, an application for judicial review of an order
or finding of the Tribunal under this Act may be made to the Federal
Court of Appeal on any of the grounds set out in subsection 18.1(4) of
the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 76
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 1988, c. 65, s. 41
- 1993, c. 44, s. 217
- 1999, c. 12, s. 36, c. 17, s. 183
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-76-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 76"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Review of Orders and Findings by Tribunal]{.HTitleText4} {#h-438857 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim review of orders by Tribunal

- **[[76.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (1.1), at any
  time after the making of an order or finding described in any of
  sections 3 to 6, the Tribunal may, on its own initiative or at the
  request of the Minister of Finance, the President or any other person
  or of any government, conduct an interim review of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the order or finding; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any aspect of the order or finding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal shall refer any portion of a request
  for interim review relating to a decision by the President setting out
  a finding of circumvention under subsection 75.1(1) or 75.4(6) to the
  President who shall make a decision relating to that portion of the
  request under section 75.4.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tribunal may re-hear any matter

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In conducting an interim review, the Tribunal may
  re-hear any matter before deciding it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal shall not conduct an interim review at
  the request of any person or government unless the person or
  government satisfies the Tribunal that the review is warranted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order if interim review not initiated

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Tribunal decides not to conduct an interim
  review at the request of a person or government, the Tribunal shall
  make an order to that effect and give reasons for it, and the Tribunal
  shall forward a copy of the order and the reasons to that person or
  government and cause notice of the order to be published in the
  [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders on completion of interim review

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal, on completion of an interim review

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} under paragraph (1)(a), shall make an order
    rescinding the order or finding or continuing it with or without
    amendment, as the circumstances require, and shall give reasons for
    making the order; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} under paragraph (1)(b), shall make any order in
    respect of the order or finding as the circumstances require, and
    shall give reasons for making the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Completion of review

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} On completion of an interim review, the Tribunal
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} forward to the President and any other persons and
    governments that are specified by the rules of the Tribunal,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} without delay after the review is completed, a
      copy of the order, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} not later than fifteen days after the date of
      the order, a copy of the reasons for the order; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} cause notice of the order to be published in the
    [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiry of order

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An order made on the completion of an interim review,
  other than an order rescinding an order or finding, expires on the day
  on which the Tribunal makes an order under subsection 76.03(12).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 12, s. 36, c. 17, s. 184
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
- 2014, c. 20, s. 437
- 2017, c. 20, s. 90
- [2022, c. 10, s. 200](#1357082-1367867){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357082-1367867.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-76.01-20180426.html "Link to previous version of section 76.01"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Review on Referral Back]{.HTitleText4} {#h-438887 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of orders by Tribunal on referral
back and re-hearing

- **[[76.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Tribunal receives notice of
  action taken under paragraph 41.1(1)(a) or (2)(a) in respect of goods
  to which an order or finding of the Tribunal, other than an order or
  finding described in any of sections 3 to 6, applies, the Tribunal
  may, on its own initiative or at the request of the Minister of
  Finance, the President or any other person or of any government,
  review the order or finding and, in conducting the review, may re-hear
  any matter before deciding it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal shall not conduct a review at the
  request of any person or government unless the person or government
  satisfies the Tribunal that a review is warranted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of orders by Tribunal on
  referral back and re-hearing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an order or finding of the Tribunal is referred
  back to the Tribunal under subsection 77.015(3) or (4), 77.019(5),
  77.15(3) or (4) or 77.19(4), the Tribunal shall review the order or
  finding and, in conducting the review, may re-hear any matter before
  deciding it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Completion of review

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On completion of a review, the Tribunal shall confirm
  the order or finding or rescind it and make any other order or finding
  with respect to the goods to which the order or finding under review
  applies as the nature of the matter may require, shall give reasons
  for the decision and, if it makes another order or finding, shall
  declare to what goods, including, if applicable, from what supplier
  and from what country of export, the order or finding applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On completion of a review, the Tribunal shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} forward to the President and the other persons and
    governments that are specified by the rules of the Tribunal and, in
    the case of a review under subsection (3), the Canadian Secretary,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} without delay after the review is completed, a
      copy of the order or finding made under subsection (4), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} not later than fifteen days after the completion
      of the review, a copy of the reasons for the decision; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} cause notice of the order or finding to be
    published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 12, s. 36, c. 17, s. 184
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
- 2014, c. 20, s. 438
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-76.02-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 76.02"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Expiry Review]{.HTitleText4} {#h-438905 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

- **[[76.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76.03
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal shall initiate an
  expiry review with respect to an order or finding described in any of
  subsections 3(1) and (2) and sections 4 to 6 before the expiry of five
  years after whichever of the following days is applicable:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if no order continuing the order or finding has
    been made under paragraph (12)(b), the day on which the order or
    finding was made; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if one or more orders continuing the order or
    finding have been made under paragraph (12)(b), the day on which the
    last order was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may terminate an expiry review at any
  time if, in the Tribunal's opinion, the review is not supported by
  domestic producers. Upon terminating a review, the Tribunal shall
  without delay cause notice of the termination to be given to the
  President and all other persons and governments specified in the rules
  of the Tribunal.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2022, c. 10, s. 201\]]{.Repealed}

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2022, c. 10, s. 201\]]{.Repealed}

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2022, c. 10, s. 201\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Upon initiating an expiry review, the Tribunal shall
  without delay

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} cause notice of the review to be given to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the President, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} all other persons and governments specified in
      the rules of the Tribunal; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2022, c. 10, s. 201\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} cause to be published in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) notice of initiation of the
    review that includes the information set out in the rules of the
    Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}President's determination and notice

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Unless the expiry review is terminated under
  subsection (2), the President shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within 150 days after the day on which the notice
    is received under subparagraph (6)(a)(i), determine whether the
    expiry of the order or finding in respect of goods of a country or
    countries is likely to result in the continuation or resumption of
    dumping or subsidizing of the goods; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide the Tribunal with notice of the
    determination without delay after making it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consequences of President's
  determination

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If the President determines that the expiry of the
  order or finding in respect of any goods is unlikely to result in a
  continuation or resumption of dumping or subsidizing, the Tribunal
  shall not take those goods into account in assessing the cumulative
  effect of dumping or subsidizing under subsection (11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consequences of President's
  determination

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If the President determines that the expiry of the
  order or finding in respect of any goods is likely to result in a
  continuation or resumption of dumping or subsidizing, the President
  shall without delay provide the Tribunal with any information and
  material with respect to the matter that is required under the rules
  of the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tribunal's determination

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} If the President makes a determination described in
  subsection (9), the Tribunal shall, within 160 days after the day on
  which that determination was received, determine whether the expiry of
  the order or finding in respect of the goods referred to in that
  subsection is likely to result in injury or retardation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of cumulative effect

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (10), the Tribunal
  shall make an assessment of the cumulative effect of the dumping or
  subsidizing of goods to which the determination of the President
  described in subsection (9) applies that are imported into Canada from
  more than one country if the Tribunal is satisfied that an assessment
  of the cumulative effect would be appropriate taking into account the
  conditions of competition between goods to which the order or finding
  applies that are imported into Canada from any of those countries and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} goods to which the order or finding applies that
    are imported into Canada from any other of those countries; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} like goods of domestic producers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of Tribunal

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal shall make an order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} rescinding the order or finding in respect of goods

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} referred to in subsection (8),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of which it determines that the
      expiry of the order or finding is unlikely to result in injury or
      retardation, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of which it terminated an expiry
      review under subsection (2); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} continuing the order or finding, with or without
    amendment, in respect of goods which it determines that the expiry
    of the order or finding is likely to result in injury or
    retardation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review period

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of conducting a review of an order
  or finding under this section the following are not to be considered:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order made by the Tribunal under section 75.3 or
    subsection 75.4(8) or 75.6(7) amending the order or finding under
    review, if that order is made on or after the day on which the
    review is initiated under subsection (1) but before the day on which
    the order of the Tribunal is made under subsection (12); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a decision or determination made by the President
    under subsection 75.1(1), 75.4(6) or 75.6(5) in respect of the order
    or finding under review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiry of anti-circumvention order

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} An order made as a result of a decision by the
  President setting out a finding of circumvention or an interim review
  decision of the President relating to a finding of circumvention,
  other than an order rescinding the extension of duties or exempting an
  exporter from the extension of duties, expires on the day on which the
  Tribunal makes an order under subsection (12).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 12, s. 36, c. 17, s. 184
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
- 2014, c. 20, ss. 439, 443
- 2016, c. 7, s. 199
- 2017, c. 20, s. 91
- [2022, c. 10, s. 201](#1357084-1367889){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357084-1367889.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-76.03-20180426.html "Link to previous version of section 76.03"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate order or finding

- **[[76.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a review under section 76.01,
  76.02 or 76.03 involves goods of more than one CUSMA country, or goods
  of one or more CUSMA countries and goods of one or more other
  countries, and the Tribunal makes another order or finding under any
  of those sections, the Tribunal shall make a separate order or finding
  under that section with respect to the goods of each CUSMA country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of subsection (3)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operation of subsection (3) is suspended during
  the period in which subsection (1) is in force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate order or finding

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a review under section 76.01, 76.02 or 76.03
  involves goods of the United States as well as goods of other
  countries and the Tribunal makes another order or finding under any of
  those sections, the Tribunal shall make a separate order or finding
  under that section with respect to the goods of the United States.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 12, s. 36
- [2020, c. 1, s. 80](#1237787-1253741){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237787-1253741.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-76.04-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 76.04"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request by Minister of Finance for review

- **[[76.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where at any time after the
  issuance, by the Dispute Settlement Body established pursuant to
  Article 2 of Annex 2 to the WTO Agreement, of a recommendation or
  ruling, the Minister of Finance considers it necessary to do so,
  having regard to the recommendation or ruling, the Minister of Finance
  may request that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the President review any decision, determination or
    re-determination or any portion of a decision, determination or
    re-determination made under this Act; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Tribunal review any order or finding described
    in any of sections 3 to 6, or any portion of such an order or
    finding and, in making the review, the Tribunal may re-hear any
    matter before deciding it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Result of review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On completion of a review under subsection (1), the
  President or the Tribunal, as the case may be, shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} continue the decision, determination,
    re-determination, order or finding without amendment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} continue the decision, determination,
    re-determination, order or finding with any amendments that the
    President or the Tribunal, as the case may be, considers necessary;
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} rescind the decision, determination,
    re-determination, order or finding and make any other decision,
    determination, re-determination, order or finding that the President
    or the Tribunal, as the case may be, considers necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a decision, determination, re-determination, order
  or finding is continued under paragraph (2)(a) or (b) or made under
  paragraph (2)(c), the President or the Tribunal, as the case may be,
  shall give reasons for doing so and shall set out to what goods,
  including, if practicable, the name of the supplier and the country of
  export, the decision, determination, re-determination, order or
  finding applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of Minister of Finance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The President or the Tribunal, as the case may be,
  shall notify the Minister of Finance of any decision, determination,
  re-determination, order or finding continued under paragraph (2)(a)
  or (b) or made under paragraph (2)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any decision, determination or re-determination
  continued by the President under paragraph (2)(b) or made by the
  President under paragraph (2)(c) is deemed to have been made under

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 41(1)(a), if the decision or
    determination was continued or made as a result of a review under
    this section of a decision of the President under that paragraph to
    cause an investigation to be terminated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 41(1)(b), if the decision or
    determination was continued or made as a result of a review under
    this section of a final determination of the President under that
    paragraph;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subsection 53(1), if the decision or determination
    was continued or made as a result of a review under this section of
    a decision of the President under that subsection to renew or not to
    renew an undertaking; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subsection 59(1), (1.1) or (2), if the
    re-determination was continued or made as a result of a review under
    this section of a re-determination by the President under either of
    those subsections.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1994, c. 47, s. 179
- 1999, c. 12, s. 37, c. 17, ss. 183, 184
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
- 2017, c. 20, s. 92
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-76.1-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 76.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Rescission of Orders and Findings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-438989 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Goods of Chile

**[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** If the Tribunal has made
an order or finding resulting in the levying of anti-dumping duties in
respect of goods of Chile that are subsequently exempted from the
application of this Act by regulations made under section 14, the
Tribunal shall rescind the order or finding to the extent that it
relates to the dumping of those goods.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 77
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 1997, c. 14, s. 92
:::

## [PART I.1]{.HLabel1}[Dispute Settlement Respecting Goods of a CUSMA Country]{.HTitleText1} {#h-438994 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-438995 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[77.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this Part,

  [[appropriate authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[appropriate authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in relation to a
      definitive decision, means either the President or the Tribunal,
      according to which made the decision; ([autorité
      compétente]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an extraordinary challenge
      committee appointed pursuant to section
      77.018; ([comité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[CUSMA country Secretary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[CUSMA country Secretary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
      secretary of the national Section of the Secretariat provided for
      in Article 30.6 of the Canada--United States--Mexico
      Agreement; ([secrétaire national]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[definitive decisions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[definitive decisions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any of the
      following decisions, final determinations, orders, findings or
      re-determinations that apply to or are made in respect of
      particular goods of a CUSMA country, but does not include any of
      them that are made for the purpose of giving effect to a decision
      of the Federal Court of Appeal or the Supreme Court of Canada
      relating to those goods:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a decision of the President under paragraph
        41(1)(a),

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a final determination of the President under
        paragraph 41(1)(b),

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order or finding of the Tribunal under
        subsection 43(1),

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a decision of the President under subsection
        53(1) to renew or not to renew an undertaking,

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a re-determination of the President under
        subsection 59(1),

      - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a re-determination of the President under
        subsection 59(3),

      - [(f.1)]{.lawlabel} a re-determination of the President under
        subsection 59(1.1),

      - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an order of the Tribunal under subsection
        76.01(4),

      - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an order of the Tribunal under subsection
        76.01(5) or 76.03(12),

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an order or finding of the Tribunal under
        subsection 76.02(4) respecting a review under subsection
        76.02(1),

      - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} an order or finding of the Tribunal under
        paragraph 76.1(2)(b) or (c), or

      - [(j)]{.lawlabel} an order or finding of the Tribunal under
        subsection 91(3). ([décisions finales]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister for
      International Trade; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[NAFTA country Secretary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[NAFTA country Secretary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      2020, c. 1, s. 82\]]{.Repealed}

  [[panel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[panel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a panel appointed pursuant to
      section 77.013; ([groupe spécial]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[rules]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[rules]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the rules of procedure, as
      amended from time to time, made under Section D of Chapter 10 of
      the Canada--United States--Mexico
      Agreement; ([règles]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[special committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[special committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a special
      committee appointed pursuant to subsection 77.023(2). ([comité
      spécial]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the event of any inconsistency between the
  provisions of this Part and the provisions of the [Federal Courts
  Act](/eng/acts/F-7), the provisions of this Part prevail to the extent
  of the inconsistency.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218

- 1999, c. 12, s. 38, c. 17, ss. 183, 184
- 2002, c. 8, ss. 172, 182
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
- 2017, c. 20, s. 93
- [2020, c. 1, s. 82](#1237795-1253749){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237795-1253749.html"
  role="button"}
- [2022, c. 10, s. 202](#1357100-1367891){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357100-1367891.html"
  role="button"}

### [Request for Review]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439024 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review of definitive decision

- **[[77.011]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.011
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister or the government of a
  CUSMA country, the goods of which are the subject of a definitive
  decision, may request, in accordance with paragraph 4 of Article 10.12
  of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement, that the definitive
  decision, in so far as it applies to goods of that CUSMA country, be
  reviewed by a panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person who, but for section 77.012, would be
  entitled to apply under the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) or
  section 96.1 of this Act, or to appeal under section 61 of this Act,
  in respect of a definitive decision may, in accordance with paragraph
  4 of Article 10.12 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement,
  file with the Canadian Secretary a request that the definitive
  decision be reviewed by a panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A request made under subsection (2) shall be deemed
  to be a request by the Minister for binational panel review within the
  meaning of paragraph 4 of Article 10.12 of the Canada--United
  States--Mexico Agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A request under subsection (1) or (2) may only be
  made within 30 days after the day on which notice of the definitive
  decision is published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) or, in the case of a
  re-determination of the President under subsection 59(1) or (3),
  within 30 days after the day on which notice of the re-determination
  is received by the government of a CUSMA country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds for request

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A request under subsection (1) or (2) for the review
  of a definitive decision may be made only on a ground set forth in
  subsection 18.1(4) of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of request for review

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} On receiving a request from the government of a CUSMA
  country under subsection (1) or on receiving a request under
  subsection (2), the Canadian Secretary shall notify the Minister and
  the appropriate CUSMA country Secretary of the request and the day on
  which it was received by the Canadian Secretary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No application or appeal

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where a request is made under subsection (1) or (2)
  for the review of a definitive decision by a panel, no person or
  government may apply under the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) or
  section 96.1 of this Act or appeal under section 61 of this Act in
  respect of the decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
- 2005, c. 38, s. 135(E)
- [2020, c. 1, s. 83](#1237816-1253759){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237816-1253759.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.011-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 77.011"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applications and appeals

- **[[77.012]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.012
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No person or government may apply
  under the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) or section 96.1 of this
  Act or appeal under section 61 of this Act in respect of a definitive
  decision

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the expiry of the period of thirty days
    after

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the definitive decision is
      published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a re-determination of the
      President under subsection 59(1), (1.1) or (3), the day on which
      notice of the re-determination is received by the government of a
      CUSMA country; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} unless the person or government has, within 20 days
    after the day on which that period commences, given notice of the
    intention to make such an application or appeal in writing to the
    Canadian Secretary and the appropriate CUSMA country Secretary and
    in the prescribed manner to any other person who, but for this
    section, would be entitled to so apply or appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period extended

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of permitting a government or person
  to apply under the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) or section 96.1
  of this Act in respect of a definitive decision after the expiration
  of the limitation period established by paragraph 4 of Article 10.12
  of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement for requesting a review
  of the decision, the limitation period referred to in subsection
  18.1(2) of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) and subsection
  96.1(3) of this Act is extended by 10 days and shall be calculated as
  commencing on the day on which the limitation period established by
  that paragraph commences.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- 1999, c. 12, s. 39, c. 17, s. 184
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
- 2005, c. 38, s. 135(E)
- [2020, c. 1, s. 84](#1237825-1253764){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237825-1253764.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.012-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 77.012"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Establishment of Panels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439053 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of panel

- **[[77.013]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.013
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On a request under section 77.011
  for the review of a definitive decision by a panel, a panel shall be
  appointed for that purpose in accordance with paragraphs 1 to 4 of
  Annex 10-B.1 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement and any
  regulations made in connection therewith.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judges may be appointed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Judges of any superior court in Canada and persons
  who are retired judges of any superior court in Canada are eligible to
  be appointed to a panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Single panel

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a request is made for the review of a final
  determination of the President under paragraph 41(1)(b) that applies
  to or is made in respect of particular goods of a CUSMA country and
  another request is made for the review of an order or finding of the
  Tribunal under subsection 43(1) that applies to or is made in respect
  of those goods, one panel may, with the consent of the Minister and
  the government of that CUSMA country, be appointed to review the final
  determination and the order or finding.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 135(E)
- 2017, c. 20, s. 94
- [2020, c. 1, s. 85](#1237842-1253768){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237842-1253768.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.013-20170622.html "Link to previous version of section 77.013"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrative record forwarded

**[[77.014]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.014 .sectionLabel}** On the
appointment of the members of a panel to review a definitive decision,
the appropriate authority shall cause a copy of the administrative
record to be forwarded in accordance with the rules.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
:::

### [Review by Panel]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439067 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conduct of review

- **[[77.015]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.015
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A panel shall conduct a review of a
  definitive decision in accordance with Section D of Chapter 10 of the
  Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement and the rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of panel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A panel has such powers, rights and privileges as are
  conferred on it by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition after review

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the review of a definitive decision,
  a panel shall determine whether the grounds on which the review was
  requested have been established and shall make an order confirming the
  decision or referring the matter back to the appropriate authority for
  reconsideration within the period specified by the panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of action of appropriate
  authority

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A panel may, on its own initiative or on a request
  made in accordance with the rules, review the action taken by the
  appropriate authority pursuant to an order under subsection (3) and
  make a further order as described in that subsection within ninety
  days after the day on which the Canadian Secretary receives notice of
  the action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A decision of a panel shall be recorded in writing
  and shall include the reasons for the decision and any dissenting or
  concurring opinions of members of the panel, and the Canadian
  Secretary shall forward, by registered mail, a copy thereof and of the
  order made pursuant to subsection (3) or (4) to the Minister, the
  government of the CUSMA country involved, the appropriate authority
  and any other person who was heard in the review and shall cause
  notice of the decision to be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 86](#1237851-1253772){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237851-1253772.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.015-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.015"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Action on Decision of Panel]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439081 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action by appropriate authority

- **[[77.016]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.016
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a panel makes an order under
  subsection 77.015(3) or (4) or takes any action under subsection
  77.019(5) referring a matter back to the appropriate authority for
  reconsideration, the appropriate authority shall, within the period
  specified by the panel, take action under this Act not inconsistent
  with the decision of the panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appropriate authority not required to
  act twice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any other provision of this Act, an
  appropriate authority is not required to act on an order under
  subsection 77.015(4), unless it requires the authority to take action
  that is different from that taken by the authority under the order
  under subsection 77.015(3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
:::

### [Extraordinary Challenge Proceeding]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439089 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for extraordinary challenge
proceeding

- **[[77.017]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.017
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within the period after a panel
  makes an order under subsection 77.015(3) or (4) prescribed by the
  rules, the Minister or the government of the CUSMA country to which
  the order relates may request, in writing to the Canadian Secretary,
  that an extraordinary challenge proceeding be commenced with respect
  to the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ground for request

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A request for an extraordinary challenge proceeding
  may be made only on a ground set forth in paragraph 13 of Article
  10.12 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of request for
  extraordinary challenge proceeding

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On receiving a request under this section made by the
  Minister, the Canadian Secretary shall notify the appropriate CUSMA
  country Secretary of the request and the day on which it was received
  by the Canadian Secretary, and on receiving a request under this
  section made by the government of a CUSMA country, the Canadian
  Secretary shall notify the Minister of the request and the day on
  which it was received by the Canadian Secretary.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 87](#1237860-1253780){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237860-1253780.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.017-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.017"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of extraordinary challenge
committee

**[[77.018]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.018 .sectionLabel}** On a request
under section 77.017 for an extraordinary challenge proceeding, an
extraordinary challenge committee shall be appointed for that purpose in
accordance with paragraph 1 of Annex 10-B.3 of the Canada--United
States--Mexico Agreement and any regulations made in connection
therewith.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 87](#1237860-1253783){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237860-1253783.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.018-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.018"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conduct of extraordinary challenge
proceeding

- **[[77.019]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.019
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A committee shall conduct an
  extraordinary challenge proceeding and make a decision in accordance
  with Annex 10-B.3 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement and
  the rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of committee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A committee has such powers, rights and privileges as
  are conferred on it by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no grounds

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a committee conducting an extraordinary
  challenge proceeding determines that the grounds in the request for
  the proceeding are not established, the committee shall deny the
  request, and the decision of the panel in respect of which the request
  was made shall stand affirmed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New panel

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where an order of a panel is set aside by a
  committee, a new panel shall, in accordance with this Part, be
  appointed and conduct a review of the definitive decision that was the
  subject of that order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action by panel

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where an order of a panel is referred back to the
  panel by a committee, the panel shall take action not inconsistent
  with the decision of the committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A decision of a committee shall be recorded in
  writing and shall include the reasons for the decision and any
  dissenting or concurring opinions of members of the committee, and the
  Canadian Secretary shall forward, by registered mail, a copy thereof
  and of the order made by the committee to the Minister, the government
  of the CUSMA country involved, the appropriate authority and any other
  person who was heard in the proceeding and shall cause notice of the
  decision to be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 88](#1237864-1253785){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237864-1253785.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.019-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.019"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders and decisions final

- **[[77.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 77.015(4) and
  section 77.019, an order or decision of a panel or committee is final
  and binding and is not subject to appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 77.015(4) and section 77.019,
  no order, decision or proceeding of a panel or committee made or
  carried on under, or purporting to be made or carried on under, this
  Act shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} questioned, reviewed, set aside, removed,
    prohibited or restrained, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} made the subject of any proceedings in, or any
    process or order of, any court, whether by way of or in the nature
    of injunction, [certiorari]{.otherLang lang="la"}, prohibition, [quo
    warranto]{.otherLang lang="la"}, declaration or otherwise,

  on any ground, including the ground that the order, decision or
  proceeding is beyond the jurisdiction of the panel or committee to
  make or carry on or that, in the course of any proceeding, the panel
  or committee for any reason exceeded or lost jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No references

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 18.3(1) of the [Federal Courts
  Act](/eng/acts/F-7) does not apply to a panel, committee or special
  committee.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.02-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.02"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Members]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439130 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Code of conduct

- **[[77.021]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.021
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every member of a panel, committee
  or special committee shall comply with the code of conduct, as amended
  from time to time, established pursuant to Article 10.17 of the
  Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure undertaking respecting
  confidential information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every member of a panel and every prescribed person
  shall sign and comply with a disclosure undertaking, in the prescribed
  form, respecting the disclosure and use of confidential, personal,
  business proprietary and other privileged or prescribed information
  made available to the member or person in proceedings under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 77.034, no action or other
  proceeding lies or shall be commenced against a member of a panel for
  or in respect of anything done or omitted to be done, or purported to
  be done or omitted to be done, under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 136(F)
- [2020, c. 1, s. 89](#1237873-1253789){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237873-1253789.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.021-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 77.021"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses of panel
members

**[[77.022]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.022 .sectionLabel}** Every member of a
panel shall be paid such remuneration and is entitled to such travel and
living expenses incurred in the performance of the member's duties under
this Part as are fixed by the Free Trade Commission established pursuant
to Article 30.1 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 90](#1237877-1253792){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237877-1253792.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.022-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.022"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Review by Special Committee]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439144 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review

- **[[77.023]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.023
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A request for a review by a special
  committee may be made to the Canadian Secretary by the government of a
  CUSMA country only with respect to an allegation referred to in
  paragraph 1 of Article 10.13 of the Canada--United States--Mexico
  Agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of special committee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On a request for a review referred to in subsection
  (1), a special committee shall be appointed for that purpose in
  accordance with Annex 10-B.3 of the Canada--United States--Mexico
  Agreement and any regulations made in connection therewith.
