[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of panel reviews and committee
proceedings

- **[[77.024]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.024
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where a
  special committee makes an affirmative finding against a CUSMA country
  pursuant to a request made by Canada in respect of an allegation
  referred to in paragraph 1 of Article 10.13 of the Canada--United
  States--Mexico Agreement, the Minister shall stay all of the following
  panel reviews and committee proceedings that were requested by the
  government or a person of that CUSMA country after the date on which
  consultations were requested under paragraph 1 of Article 10.13 of the
  Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} panel reviews under section 77.011; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} committee proceedings under section 77.017.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a panel
  review or committee proceeding that was requested more than one
  hundred and fifty days prior to the affirmative finding by the special
  committee.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 92](#1237885-1253802){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237885-1253802.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.024-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.024"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay on request

**[[77.025]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.025 .sectionLabel}** Where a special
committee makes an affirmative finding against Canada pursuant to a
request made by the government of a CUSMA country, the government of
that CUSMA country may request that the Minister stay all of the
following panel reviews and committee proceedings that were requested by
the government or a person of that CUSMA country, and where such a
request for a stay is made the Minister shall stay all such reviews and
proceedings:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} panel reviews under section 77.011; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} committee proceedings under section 77.017.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 93](#1237889-1253807){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237889-1253807.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.025-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.025"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When stay becomes effective

**[[77.026]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.026 .sectionLabel}** Where the
Minister stays panel reviews and committee proceedings, the stay shall
become effective

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the stay is made under section 77.024, on the
  day following the date on which the special committee made the
  affirmative finding; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the stay is made under section 77.025, on the
  day following the date on which the request for the stay was made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of time periods

**[[77.027]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.027 .sectionLabel}** Where a special
committee makes an affirmative finding against Canada or a CUSMA country
pursuant to a request made by the government of a CUSMA country or
Canada in respect of an allegation referred to in paragraph 1 of Article
10.13 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement, the following time
periods shall not run unless and until resumed in accordance with
section 77.033:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the time periods provided for in subsection 77.011(4)
  for requesting a panel review and in subsection 77.017(1) for
  requesting committee proceedings in respect of goods of that CUSMA
  country; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the time periods provided in the [Federal Courts
  Act](/eng/acts/F-7), and in section 61 and subsection 96.1(3) of this
  Act, for appealing, or for requesting judicial review of, any
  determination, re-determination, decision or order referred to in the
  definition [[definitive decision]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection
  77.01(1) in respect of goods of that CUSMA country.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
- [2020, c. 1, s. 94](#1237893-1253812){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237893-1253812.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of panel process

- **[[77.028]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.028
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may suspend the
  operation of Article 10.12 of the Canada--United States--Mexico
  Agreement with respect to goods of a CUSMA country

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at any time after the expiration of 60 days, but
    not later than 90 days, following an affirmative finding against the
    CUSMA country by a special committee requested by Canada under
    paragraph 2 of Article 10.13 of the Canada--United States--Mexico
    Agreement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at any time where the government of the CUSMA
    country has suspended the operation of Article 10.12 of the
    Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement with respect to goods of
    Canada following an affirmative finding by a special committee
    against Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of suspension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister suspends the operation of Article
  10.12 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement under
  subsection (1) with respect to goods of a CUSMA country, the Canadian
  Secretary shall forward a written notice of the suspension to the
  CUSMA country Secretary of that CUSMA country and shall publish a
  notice of the suspension in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 94](#1237893-1253820){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237893-1253820.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.028-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.028"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of benefits

- **[[77.029]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.029
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council, on the
  recommendation of the Minister of Finance and the Minister, may, by
  order, at any time after the expiration of 60 days, but in no case
  later than 90 days, following an affirmative finding against a CUSMA
  country by a special committee requested by Canada under paragraph 2
  of Article 10.13 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement,
  suspend the application to that CUSMA country of such benefits under
  that Agreement as the Governor in Council considers appropriate in the
  circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of suspending the application to a
  CUSMA country of benefits under subsection (1), the Governor in
  Council may do any one or more of the following things:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} suspend rights or privileges granted by Canada to
    that country or to goods, service providers, suppliers, investors or
    investments of that country under the Canada--United States--Mexico
    Agreement or an Act of Parliament;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} modify or suspend the application of any federal
    law with respect to that country or to goods, service providers,
    suppliers, investors or investments of that country;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} extend the application of any federal law to that
    country or to goods, service providers, suppliers, investors or
    investments of that country; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} generally take such action as the Governor in
    Council considers necessary for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless revoked, an order made under subsection (1)
  shall have effect for such period as is specified in the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[federal law]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  means the whole or any portion of any Act of Parliament or regulation,
  order or other instrument issued, made or established in the exercise
  of a power conferred by or under an Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order not a statutory instrument

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) is not a statutory
  instrument for the purposes of the [Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action consistent with determination

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Whenever, after an order is made under subsection
  (1), the special committee referred to in that subsection makes a
  determination pursuant to paragraph 10(a) of Article 10.13 of the
  Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement, the Governor in Council shall
  take action consistent with that determination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 95](#1237897-1253826){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237897-1253826.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.029-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.029"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Only one section applies

**[[77.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.03 .sectionLabel}** Where the operation
of Article 10.12 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement is
suspended under section 77.028 in respect of a CUSMA country, benefits
under paragraph 2 of Article 10.13 of that Agreement may not be
suspended under section 77.029 in respect of that CUSMA country, and if
benefits under paragraph 2 of Article 10.13 of that Agreement are
suspended under section 77.029 in respect of a CUSMA country, the
operation of Article 10.12 of that Agreement may not be suspended under
section 77.028 in respect of that CUSMA country.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 96](#1237914-1253829){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237914-1253829.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-77.03-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.03"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral to Federal Court of Appeal

- **[[77.031]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.031
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister suspends the
  operation of Article 10.12 of the Canada--United States--Mexico
  Agreement under paragraph 77.028(1)(a) and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where any panel review is stayed under subsection
    77.024(1), the Minister, the government of the CUSMA country, or any
    party to the stayed panel review may, within 30 days after the date
    of the suspension, apply to the Federal Court of Appeal for review
    of the definitive decision that has been the subject of the panel
    review, on any grounds set out in subsection 18.1(4) of the [Federal
    Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where any committee proceeding is stayed under
    subsection 77.024(1), the Minister, the government of the CUSMA
    country, or any party to the stayed committee proceeding may, within
    30 days after the date of the suspension, apply to the Federal Court
    of Appeal for review of the definitive decision that has been the
    subject of the original panel decision reviewed by the committee, on
    any grounds set out in subsection 18.1(4) of the [Federal Courts
    Act](/eng/acts/F-7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the government of a CUSMA country suspends the
  operation of Article 10.12 of the Canada--United States--Mexico
  Agreement with respect to goods of Canada under paragraph 8 of Article
  10.13 of that Agreement and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where any panel review is stayed under section
    77.025, the government of the CUSMA country, or persons of that
    CUSMA country who were party to the stayed panel review may, within
    30 days after the date of the suspension, apply to the Federal Court
    of Appeal for review of the definitive decision that has been the
    subject of the panel review, on any grounds set out in subsection
    18.1(4) of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where any committee proceeding is stayed under
    section 77.025, the government of the CUSMA country, or persons of
    that CUSMA country who were party to the stayed committee proceeding
    may, within 30 days after the date of the suspension, apply to the
    Federal Court of Appeal for review of the definitive decision that
    has been the subject of the original panel decision reviewed by the
    committee, on any grounds set out in subsection 18.1(4) of the
    [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (1) and (2), where
  any application has been made to the Federal Court of Appeal for the
  review of any definitive decision, that definitive decision may not be
  subsequently reviewed by a panel or committee if the suspension of
  Article 10.12 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement is
  terminated pursuant to section 77.032.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182

  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237914-1253841.html"
  role="button"}
:::

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of suspension

**[[77.032]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.032 .sectionLabel}** The Minister
shall terminate any suspension effected under subsection 77.028(1) if a
special committee reconvened pursuant to paragraph 10 of Article 10.13
of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement determines that the
problems in respect of which the special committee's affirmative finding
was based have been corrected.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 96](#1237914-1253844){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237914-1253844.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.032-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.032"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resumption

**[[77.033]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.033 .sectionLabel}** All panel reviews
and committee proceedings stayed under subsection 77.024(1) or section
77.025 and any running of the time periods suspended under section
77.027 shall resume

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the operation of Article 10.12 of the
  Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement is not suspended under
  paragraph 77.028(1)(a), on the expiration of 90 days after the date on
  which an affirmative finding was made or on such earlier day as the
  Minister may specify; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where benefits are suspended under section 77.029.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 96](#1237914-1253849){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237914-1253849.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-77.033-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.033"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Offence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439237 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

- **[[77.034]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.034
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who
  contravenes or fails to comply with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a disclosure undertaking under subsection
    77.021(2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the rules respecting the disclosure and use of
    confidential, personal, business proprietary or other privileged or
    prescribed information; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a disclosure order or protective order covering
    personal, business proprietary or other privileged or prescribed
    information made under the law of any CUSMA country giving effect to
    the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who commits an offence under subsection
  (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to a
    fine not exceeding one million dollars; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
    conviction and liable to a fine not exceeding one hundred thousand
    dollars.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No proceedings for an offence under this section
  shall be instituted without the consent in writing of the Attorney
  General of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 97](#1237918-1253851){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237918-1253851.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.034-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.034"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439252 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[77.035]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.035 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister and the Minister of
Finance, make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} conferring on a panel, committee or special committee
  such powers, rights and privileges as the Governor in Council deems
  necessary for giving effect to Section D of Chapter 10 of the
  Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement and the rules, including
  powers, rights and privileges of a superior court of record;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorizing a designated officer, or an officer of a
  designated class of officers, employed in or occupying a position of
  responsibility in the service of Her Majesty to perform duties or
  functions of the Minister under this Part;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for carrying out and giving effect to paragraphs 1 to
  4 of Annex 10-B.1 of the Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement and
  paragraph 1 of Annex 10-B.3 of that Agreement: and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} generally for carrying out the purposes and
  provisions of this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 98](#1237922-1253854){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237922-1253854.html"
  role="button"}

Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/)

**[[77.036]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.036 .sectionLabel}** The rules, the
code of conduct established pursuant to Article 10.17 of the
Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement and any amendments made to the
rules or code shall be published in the [Canada
Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

### [Application of Acts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439265 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[77.037]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.037 .sectionLabel}** If one of the
following provisions comes into force after the coming into force of
this section, then that provision shall not be applied in respect of
goods of a CUSMA country unless an Act of Parliament expressly declares
that the provision applies in respect of goods of that CUSMA country:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a provision of an Act to amend this Act;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a provision of any other Act of Parliament respecting
  the imposition of anti-dumping or countervailing duties; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a provision that amends a provision of an Act of
  Parliament providing for judicial review of a definitive decision or
  setting forth the grounds for such a review.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
- [2020, c. 1, s. 100](#1237935-1253863){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237935-1253863.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.037-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.037"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of Part II

**[[77.038]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.038 .sectionLabel}** The operation of
Part II is suspended during the period in which this Part is in force.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 218
:::

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Dispute Settlement Respecting Goods of the United States]{.HTitleText1} {#h-439278 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439279 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[77.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this Part,

  [[American Secretary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[American Secretary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the secretary of
      the United States section of the Secretariat provided for by
      Article 1909 of the Free Trade Agreement; ([secrétaire
      américain]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[appropriate authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[appropriate authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in relation to a
      definitive decision, means either the President or the Tribunal,
      according to which made the decision; ([autorité
      compétente]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an extraordinary challenge
      committee appointed pursuant to section
      77.18; ([comité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[definitive decision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[definitive decision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a decision of the President under paragraph
        41(1)(a),

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a final determination of the President under
        paragraph 41(1)(b),

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order or finding of the Tribunal under
        subsection 43(1),

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a decision of the President under subsection
        53(1) to renew or not to renew an undertaking,

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a re-determination of the President under
        subsection 59(1),

      - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a re-determination of the President under
        subsection 59(3),

      - [(f.1)]{.lawlabel} a re-determination of the President under
        subsection 59(1.1),

      - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an order of the Tribunal under subsection
        76.01(4),

      - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an order of the Tribunal under subsection
        76.01(5) or 76.03(12),

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an order or finding of the Tribunal under
        subsection 76.02(4) respecting a review under subsection
        76.02(1),

      - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} an order or finding of the Tribunal under
        paragraph 76.1(2)(b) or (c), or

      - [(j)]{.lawlabel} an order or finding of the Tribunal under
        subsection 91(3)

      that applies to or in respect of particular goods of the United
      States, but does not include any such determination,
      re-determination, decision, order or finding that is made for the
      purpose of giving effect to a decision of the Federal Court of
      Appeal or the Supreme Court of Canada relating to those
      goods; ([décisions finales]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister for
      International Trade; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[panel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[panel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a panel appointed pursuant to
      section 77.13; ([groupe spécial]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[rules]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[rules]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the rules of procedure, as
      amended from time to time, made pursuant to Chapter Nineteen of
      the Free Trade Agreement; ([règles]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Secretariat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Secretariat]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canadian Secretariat
      established by section 77.23. ([Secrétariat]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the event of any inconsistency between the
  provisions of this Part and the provisions of the [Federal Courts
  Act](/eng/acts/F-7), the provisions of this Part prevail to the extent
  of the inconsistency.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
- 1994, c. 47, s. 181
- 1999, c. 12, s. 40, c. 17, ss. 183, 184
- 2002, c. 8, ss. 173, 182
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134

  role="button"}
:::

### [Request for Review]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439308 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review of definitive decision

- **[[77.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister or the United States
  government may request, in accordance with paragraph 4 of Article 1904
  of the Free Trade Agreement, that a definitive decision be reviewed by
  a panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On a request made to the Canadian Secretary by any
  person who, but for section 77.12, would be entitled to apply under
  section 28 of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) or section 96.1
  of this Act or to appeal under section 61 of this Act in respect of a
  definitive decision, the Minister shall request, in accordance with
  paragraph 4 of Article 1904 of the Free Trade Agreement, that the
  definitive decision be reviewed by a panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No request shall be made to the Canadian Secretary
  under subsection (2) more than twenty-five days after the day on which
  notice of the definitive decision is published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) or, in the case of a
  re-determination of the President under subsection 59(1) or (3), the
  day on which notice of the re-determination is received by the United
  States government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds for request

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A request by the Minister for the review of a
  definitive decision may be made only on a ground set forth in
  subsection 28(1) of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of request for review

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On receiving a request under this section made by the
  Minister, the Canadian Secretary shall notify the American Secretary
  of the request and the day on which it was received by the Canadian
  Secretary, and on receiving a request under this section made by the
  United States government, the Canadian Secretary shall notify the
  Minister of the request and the day on which it was received by the
  Canadian Secretary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No application or appeal

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where a request is made by the Minister or the United
  States government for the review of a definitive decision by a panel,
  no person or government may apply under section 18 or 28 of the
  [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) or section 96.1 of this Act or
  appeal under section 61 of this Act in respect of the decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
- 2005, c. 38, s. 135(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.11-20030702.html "Link to previous version of section 77.11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applications and appeals

- **[[77.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No person or government may apply
  under section 18 or 28 of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) or
  section 96.1 of this Act or appeal under section 61 of this Act in
  respect of a definitive decision

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the expiry of the period of thirty days
    after

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the definitive decision is
      published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a re-determination of the
      President under subsection 59(1), (1.1) or (3), the day on which
      notice of the re-determination is received by the United States;
      and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} unless the person or government has, within twenty
    days after the day on which that period commences, given notice of
    the intention to make such an application or appeal in writing to
    the Canadian Secretary and the American Secretary and in the
    prescribed manner to any other person who, but for this section,
    would be entitled to so apply or appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period extended

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of permitting a government or person
  to apply under section 28 of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7)
  or section 96.1 of this Act in respect of a definitive decision after
  the expiration of the limitation period established by paragraph 4 of
  Article 1904 of the Free Trade Agreement for requesting a review of
  the decision, the ten day limitation period referred to in subsection
  28(2) of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) and subsection
  96.1(3) of this Act is extended by thirty days and shall be calculated
  as commencing on the day on which the limitation period established by
  that paragraph commences.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
- 1999, c. 12, s. 41, c. 17, s. 184
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
- 2005, c. 38, s. 135(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.12-20030702.html "Link to previous version of section 77.12"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Establishment of Panels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439335 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of panel

- **[[77.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On a request under section 77.11 for
  the review of a definitive decision by a panel, a panel shall be
  appointed for that purpose in accordance with paragraphs 1 to 4 of
  Annex 1901.2 to Chapter Nineteen of the Free Trade Agreement and any
  regulations made in connection therewith.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Single panel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a request is made for the review of a final
  determination of the President under paragraph 41(1)(b) that applies
  to or in respect of particular goods of the United States and another
  request is made for the review of an order or finding of the Tribunal
  under subsection 43(1) that applies to or in respect of those goods,
  one panel may, with the consent of the Minister and the United States
  government, be appointed to review the final determination and the
  order or finding.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 135(E)
- 2017, c. 20, s. 96
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.13-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 77.13"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrative record forwarded

**[[77.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.14 .sectionLabel}** On the appointment
of the members of a panel to review a definitive decision, the
appropriate authority shall cause a copy of the administrative record to
be forwarded in accordance with the rules.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
- 2014, c. 20, s. 440(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.14-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.14"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Review by Panel]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439347 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conduct of review

- **[[77.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A panel shall conduct a review of a
  definitive decision in accordance with Chapter Nineteen of the Free
  Trade Agreement and the rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of panel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A panel has such powers, rights and privileges as are
  conferred on it by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition after review

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the review of a definitive decision,
  a panel shall determine whether the grounds on which the review was
  requested have been established and shall make an order confirming the
  decision or referring the matter back to the appropriate authority for
  reconsideration within the period specified by the panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of action of appropriate
  authority

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A panel may, on its own initiative or on a request
  made in accordance with the rules, review the action taken by the
  appropriate authority pursuant to an order under subsection (3) and
  make a further order as described in that subsection within ninety
  days after the day on which the Canadian Secretary receives notice of
  the action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A decision of a panel shall be recorded in writing
  and shall include the reasons for the decision and any dissenting or
  concurring opinions of members of the panel, and the Canadian
  Secretary shall forward, by registered mail, a copy thereof and of the
  order made pursuant to subsection (3) or (4) to the Minister, the
  United States government, the appropriate authority and any other
  person who was heard in the review and shall cause notice of the
  decision to be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

### [Action on Decision of Panel]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439361 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action by appropriate authority

- **[[77.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a panel makes an order under
  subsection 77.15(3) or (4) referring a matter back to the appropriate
  authority for reconsideration, the appropriate authority shall, within
  the period specified by the panel, take action under this Act not
  inconsistent with the decision of the panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appropriate authority not required to
  act twice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any other provision of this Act, an
  appropriate authority is not required to act on an order under
  subsection 77.15(4), unless it requires the authority to take action
  that is different from that taken by the authority under the order
  under subsection 77.15(3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

### [Extraordinary Challenge Proceeding]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439369 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for extraordinary challenge
proceeding

- **[[77.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within the period after a panel
  makes an order under subsection 77.15(3) or (4) prescribed by the
  rules, the Minister or the United States government may request, in
  writing to the Canadian Secretary, that an extraordinary challenge
  proceeding be commenced with respect to the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ground for request

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A request for an extraordinary challenge proceeding
  may be made only on a ground set forth in paragraph 13 of Article 1904
  of the Free Trade Agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of request for
  extraordinary challenge proceeding

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On receiving a request under this section made by the
  Minister, the Canadian Secretary shall notify the American Secretary
  of the request and the day on which it was received by the Canadian
  Secretary, and on receiving a request under this section made by the
  United States government, the Canadian Secretary shall notify the
  Minister of the request and the day on which it was received by the
  Canadian Secretary.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of extraordinary challenge
committee

**[[77.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.18 .sectionLabel}** On a request under
section 77.17 for an extraordinary challenge proceeding, an
extraordinary challenge committee shall be appointed for that purpose in
accordance with paragraph 1 of Annex 1904.13 to Chapter Nineteen of the
Free Trade Agreement and any regulations made in connection therewith.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conduct of extraordinary challenge
proceeding

- **[[77.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A committee shall conduct an
  extraordinary challenge proceeding and make a decision in accordance
  with Annex 1904.13 to Chapter Nineteen of the Free Trade Agreement and
  the rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of committee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A committee has such powers, rights and privileges as
  are conferred on it by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New panel

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an order of a panel is set aside by a
  committee, a new panel shall, in accordance with this Part, be
  appointed and conduct a review of the definitive decision that was the
  subject of that order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action by panel

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where an order of a panel is referred back to the
  panel by a committee, the panel shall take action not inconsistent
  with the decision of the committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A decision of a committee shall be recorded in
  writing and shall include the reasons for the decision and any
  dissenting or concurring opinions of members of the committee, and the
  Canadian Secretary shall forward, by registered mail, a copy thereof
  and of the order made by the committee to the Minister, the United
  States government, the appropriate authority and any other person who
  was heard in the proceeding and shall cause notice of the decision to
  be published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders and decisions final

- **[[77.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 77.15(4) and
  section 77.17, an order or decision of a panel or committee is final
  and binding and is not subject to appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 77.15(4) and section 77.17, no
  order, decision or proceeding of a panel or committee made or carried
  on under, or purporting to be made or carried on under, this Act shall
  be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} questioned, reviewed, set aside, removed,
    prohibited or restrained, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} made the subject of any proceedings in, or any
    process or order of, any court, whether by way of or in the nature
    of injunction, [certiorari]{.otherLang lang="la"}, prohibition, [quo
    warranto]{.otherLang lang="la"}, declaration or otherwise,

  on any ground, including the ground that the order, decision or
  proceeding is beyond the jurisdiction of the panel or committee to
  make or carry on or that, in the course of any proceeding, the panel
  or committee for any reason exceeded or lost jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No references

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 28(4) of the [Federal Courts
  Act](/eng/acts/F-7) does not apply to a panel or committee.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.2-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Members]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439408 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Code of conduct

- **[[77.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every member of a panel and every
  member of a committee shall comply with the code of conduct, as
  amended from time to time, established pursuant to Article 1910 of the
  Free Trade Agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure undertaking respecting
  confidential information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every member of a panel and every prescribed person
  shall sign and comply with a disclosure undertaking, in the prescribed
  form, respecting the disclosure and use of confidential, personal,
  business proprietary and other privileged information made available
  to the member or person in proceedings under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 77.26, no action or other
  proceeding lies or shall be commenced against a member of a panel for
  or in respect of anything done or omitted to be done, or purported to
  be done or omitted to be done, under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 136(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.21-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses of panel
members

**[[77.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.22 .sectionLabel}** Every member of a
panel shall be paid such remuneration and is entitled to such travel and
living expenses incurred in the performance of the member's duties under
this Part as are fixed by the Canada-United States Trade Commission
established pursuant to the Free Trade Agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

### [Secretariat]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439422 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment of Canadian Secretariat

**[[77.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.23 .sectionLabel}** There is hereby
established a secretariat, to be called the Canadian Secretariat, for
the purpose of facilitating the implementation of Chapter Nineteen of
the Free Trade Agreement and the work of panels and committees.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Secretary

- **[[77.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.24
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There shall be a Secretary of the
  Secretariat to be appointed by the Governor in Council, on the
  recommendation of the Minister, to hold office for a term not
  exceeding five years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Salary and expenses

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Canadian Secretary shall be paid such salary or
  other remuneration and expenses as may be fixed by the Governor in
  Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity of Secretary

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the event of the absence or incapacity of the
  Canadian Secretary or if the office of Canadian Secretary is vacant,
  the Governor in Council may appoint another person, on such terms and
  conditions as the Governor in Council deems appropriate, to act as
  Canadian Secretary and a person so acting shall have all the powers,
  duties and functions of the Canadian Secretary under this Part and be
  paid such salary or other remuneration and expenses as may be fixed by
  the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Superannuation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The provisions of the [Public Service Superannuation
  Act](/eng/acts/P-36), other than those related to tenure of office,
  apply to the Canadian Secretary, except that a person appointed as
  Canadian Secretary from outside the public service may, by notice in
  writing given to the President of the Treasury Board not more than
  sixty days after the date of appointment, elect to participate in the
  [Diplomatic Service (Special) Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/D-2), in
  which case the provisions of that Act, other than those relating to
  tenure of office, apply to the Canadian Secretary from the date of
  appointment and the provisions of the [Public Service Superannuation
  Act](/eng/acts/P-36) do not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief executive officer

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Canadian Secretary is the chief executive officer
  of the Secretariat and has supervision over and direction of the work
  and staff of the Secretariat.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
- 2003, c. 22, s. 225(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77.24-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 77.24"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff

**[[77.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.25 .sectionLabel}** Such officers,
clerks and employees as are required for the proper conduct of the work
of the Secretariat shall be appointed in accordance with the [Public
Service Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

### [Offence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439444 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

- **[[77.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who
  contravenes or fails to comply with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a disclosure undertaking under subsection 77.21(2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the rules respecting the disclosure and use of
    confidential, personal, business proprietary or other privileged
    information; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a protective order covering business proprietary
    and other privileged information made under the American law giving
    effect to the Free Trade Agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who commits an offence under subsection
  (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to a
    fine not exceeding one million dollars; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
    conviction and liable to a fine not exceeding one hundred thousand
    dollars.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No proceedings for an offence under this section
  shall be instituted without the consent in writing of the Attorney
  General of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439459 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[77.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.27 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister and the Minister of
Finance, make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} conferring on a panel or committee such powers,
  rights and privileges as the Governor in Council deems necessary for
  giving effect to Chapter Nineteen of the Free Trade Agreement and the
  rules, including powers, rights and privileges of a superior court of
  record;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorizing a designated officer, or an officer of a
  designated class of officers, employed in or occupying a position of
  responsibility in the service of Her Majesty to perform duties or
  functions of the Minister under this Part;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for carrying out and giving effect to paragraphs 1 to
  4 of Annex 1901.2, and paragraph 1 of Annex 1904.13, to Chapter
  Nineteen of the Free Trade Agreement; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} generally for carrying out the purposes and
  provisions of this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication in [Canada
Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/)

**[[77.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.28 .sectionLabel}** The rules, the code
of conduct established pursuant to Article 1910 of the Free Trade
Agreement and any amendments made to the rules or code shall be
published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

### [Application of Acts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439472 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[77.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.29 .sectionLabel}** No provision

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} of an Act to amend this Act,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} of any other Act of Parliament respecting the
  imposition of anti-dumping or countervailing duties, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} amending a provision of an Act of Parliament
  providing for judicial review of a definitive decision or setting
  forth the grounds for such a review

that comes into force after the coming into force of this section shall
be applied in respect of goods of the United States, unless it is
expressly declared by an Act of Parliament that the provision applies in
respect of goods of the United States.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1988, c. 65, s. 42
:::

## [PART III]{.HLabel1}[General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-439481 .Part}

### [Provision of Evidence to President]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439482 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}President may require evidence to be
provided

- **[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in any proceeding undertaken by the President after
    notice has been given that the complaint is properly documented but
    before the initiation of an investigation or in any investigation
    under this Act respecting the dumping or subsidizing of goods, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in relation to the sale of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any goods to an importer in Canada, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any goods located or in the course of production
      out of Canada,

    that are of the same description as goods to which an order or
    finding of the Tribunal described in section 3, 5 or 6 applies and
    that will or may be imported into Canada,

  the President believes on reasonable grounds that any person in Canada
  is able to provide evidence relevant to any proceedings undertaken by
  the President before the initiation of an investigation, to the
  investigation or to the making, for the purpose of facilitating the
  administration or enforcement of this Act, of an estimate of the duty
  that will or may be payable on the goods when imported into Canada,
  the President may, by notice in writing, require the person to provide
  the President, under oath or otherwise, with the evidence referred to
  in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to provide evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, by notice given pursuant to subsection (1),
  the President requires any person to provide evidence, he shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include in the notice sufficient information for
    the person to identify the evidence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specify in the notice the time within which and the
    manner and form in which the evidence is to be provided; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} include with the notice a copy or summary of this
    section and sections 82 to 85.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence or statement to be provided

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a person is required by notice given pursuant
  to subsection (1) to provide the President with evidence, the person
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if it is reasonably practicable for the person to
    do so, provide the evidence in accordance with the notice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if it is reasonably practicable for the person to
    provide a part only of the evidence in accordance with the notice,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} so provide that part of the evidence, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} provide the President with a written statement
      under oath identifying the remainder of the evidence and
      specifying the reason why it is not reasonably practicable for the
      person to provide the remainder of the evidence in accordance with
      the notice; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if it is not reasonably practicable for the person
    to provide the evidence in accordance with the notice, provide the
    President with a statement under oath so stating and specifying the
    reason why it is not reasonably practicable to so provide the
    evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No oral evidence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section shall be construed as
  authorizing the President to require any person to provide evidence
  orally.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where, pursuant to paragraph (2)(b), the President
  specifies the time within which evidence is to be provided, the
  President may, either before or after the expiration of that time,
  extend the time within which the evidence is to be provided.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 78
- 1999, c. 12, s. 42, c. 17, ss. 183, 184
- 2005, c. 38, ss. 134, 135(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-78-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 78"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of evidence as confidential

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person who provides the President with evidence pursuant to
  subsection 78(3) wishes some or all of the evidence to be kept
  confidential, the person shall submit, at the time the evidence is
  provided, a statement designating as confidential the evidence that he
  wishes to be kept confidential, together with an explanation of why he
  designated that evidence as confidential.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summary or statement to be provided

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, pursuant to subsection (1), a person submits
  to the President a statement designating evidence as confidential,
  together with the explanation referred to in that subsection, the
  person shall submit to the President, at the same time, a summary of
  the evidence designated as confidential in sufficient detail to convey
  a reasonable understanding of the evidence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 79
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 135(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-79-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 79"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Collection of Duty]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439517 .Subheading}

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, R.S., 1985,
c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 209\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of duties from person other than
importer

- **[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding anything in this
  Act, if any duty payable under this Act in respect of goods has not
  been paid within thirty days after a demand for payment of the duty
  has been made under this Act, the President may, by notice in writing,
  require any person in Canada to whom the goods are sold to pay a sum
  in respect of the duty not exceeding the amount of the duty payable in
  respect of the goods sold to that person, which sum is, after the
  notice has been given, a debt due and payable to Her Majesty by that
  person and may be recovered at any time by action in any court of
  competent jurisdiction, together with costs of the action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recourse under [Customs
  Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an amount that is less than the duty payable in
  respect of goods imported into Canada is recovered from a person
  pursuant to subsection (1), such recovery is without prejudice to any
  recourse available to Her Majesty under the [Customs
  Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) with respect to the remainder of the duty
  payable.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 81
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 210
- 1999, c. 12, s. 43, c. 17, s. 184
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
:::

[Previous
Version](section-81-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 81"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Disclosure of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-439526 .Subheading}

Definition of [[information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** In sections 83 to 87,
[[information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes evidence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 25, s. 82
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to be disclosed

**[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** Where information is
provided to the President for the purposes of any proceedings under this
Act, every party to the proceedings has, unless the information is
information to which subsection 84(1) applies, a right, on request, to
examine the information during normal business hours and a right, on
payment of the prescribed fee, to be provided with copies of any such
information that is in documentary form or that is in any other form in
which it may be readily and accurately copied.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 83
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-83-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 83"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to be disclosed

**[[83.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83.1 .sectionLabel}** Where information is
provided to the President for the purposes of any proceedings under this
Act in respect of goods of a CUSMA country, the President shall, on
receipt of a request from the government of that CUSMA country, provide
that government with copies of any such information that is requested
that is in documentary form or that is in any other form in which it may
be readily and accurately copied, unless the information is information
to which subsection 84(1) applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 219
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
- [2020, c. 1, s. 101](#1237939-1253866){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237939-1253866.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-83.1-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 83.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information not to be disclosed

- **[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designates information as confidential pursuant to
    paragraph 85(1)(a), or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} submits to the President, with respect to evidence,
    in this section referred to as "information", provided by him
    pursuant to subsection 78(3), the statement and explanation referred
    to in subsection 79(1),

  and that designation or submission, as the case may be, is not
  withdrawn by the person, no person employed in the federal public
  administration who comes into possession of that information while he
  is so employed shall, either before or after he ceases to be so
  employed, knowingly disclose that information, or knowingly allow it
  to be disclosed, to any other person in any manner that is calculated
  or likely to make it available for the use of any business competitor
  or rival of any person to whose business or affairs the information
  relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any summary of information or statement referred to
    in paragraph 85(1)(b) or any summary referred to in subsection
    79(2); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure by the President of information for
    the purposes of proceedings before a panel or the Appellate Body
    established under the Understanding on Rules and Procedures
    Governing the Settlement of Disputes set out in Annex 2 to the WTO
    Agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure to counsel

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), information to which
  that subsection applies that has been provided to the President in any
  proceedings under this Act shall, on written request and on payment of
  the prescribed fee, be disclosed by the President, in the manner and
  at the time specified by the President, to counsel for any party to
  those proceedings or to other proceedings under this Act arising out
  of those proceedings for use, notwithstanding any other Act or law, by
  that counsel only in those proceedings, subject to any conditions that
  the President considers reasonably necessary or desirable to ensure
  that the information will not, without the written consent of the
  person who submitted it to the President, be disclosed to any person
  by counsel in any manner that is calculated or likely to make it
  available to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any party to the proceedings or other proceedings,
    including a party who is represented by that counsel; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any business competitor or rival of any person to
    whose business or affairs the information relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The President may not disclose information under
  subsection (3) if the President is satisfied that the disclosure might
  result in material harm to the business or affairs of the person who
  designated the information as confidential under paragraph 85(1)(a).

- Definition of [[counsel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In subsection (3), [[counsel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm},
  in relation to a party to proceedings under this Act, includes any
  person, other than a director, servant or employee of the party, who
  acts in the proceedings on behalf of the party.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 84
- R.S., 1985, c. 47 (4th Supp.), s. 52
- 1999, c. 12, s. 44, c. 17, ss. 183, 184
- 2003, c. 22, s. 224(E)
- 2005, c. 38, ss. 134, 135(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-84-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 84"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of information as
confidential

- **[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person who provides information to the President for the purposes of
  proceedings under this Act wishes some or all of the information to be
  kept confidential, the person shall submit, at the time the
  information is provided,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a statement designating as confidential the
    information that he wishes to be kept confidential, together with an
    explanation of why he designated that information as confidential;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a non-confidential edited version or
    non-confidential summary of the information designated as
    confidential pursuant to paragraph (a) in sufficient detail to
    convey a reasonable understanding of the substance of the
    information or a statement

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that such a non-confidential edited version or
      non-confidential summary cannot be made, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} that such a non-confidential edited version or
      non-confidential summary would disclose facts that the person has
      a proper reason for wishing to keep confidential,

    together with an explanation that justifies the making of any such
    statement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who designates information as confidential
  pursuant to paragraph (1)(a) fails to comply with paragraph (1)(b)
  where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person does not provide a non-confidential
    edited version, a non-confidential summary or a statement referred
    to in paragraph (1)(b);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person provides a non-confidential edited
    version or a non-confidential summary of the information designated
    as confidential pursuant to paragraph (1)(a), but the President is
    satisfied that it does not comply with paragraph (1)(b);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person provides a statement referred to in
    paragraph (1)(b), but does not provide an explanation that justifies
    the making of the statement; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the person provides a statement referred to in
    paragraph (1)(b), but the President is satisfied that the
    explanation given as justification for the making of the statement
    does not justify the making thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 85
- 1994, c. 47, s. 182
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-85-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 85"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where there has been failure to comply

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person has designated information as confidential pursuant to
  paragraph 85(1)(a) and the President considers that the designation is
  warranted, but the person has failed to comply with paragraph
  85(1)(b), the President shall cause the person to be informed of that
  failure, of the ground on which he has so failed and of the
  application of subsection 87(3) if the person fails to take, within
  the time limited therefor by or pursuant to that subsection, such
  action as it is necessary for him to take in order to comply with
  paragraph 85(1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where President considers designation
  unwarranted

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, pursuant to paragraph 85(1)(a), a person has
  designated information as confidential and the President considers
  that, because of its nature, extent, availability from other sources
  or the failure of the person to provide any explanation of why he
  designated it as confidential, the designation of that information as
  confidential is unwarranted, the President shall cause the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to be notified of the fact that the President
    considers the designation of that information as confidential
    unwarranted and of the President's reasons for so considering; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the person has failed to comply with
    paragraph 85(1)(b), to be informed as provided in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 86
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-86-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 86"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of designation or submission
of explanation

- **[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person is notified pursuant to paragraph 86(2)(a) with respect to
  any information that he has designated as confidential pursuant to
  paragraph 85(1)(a), the person may, within fifteen days after being so
  notified,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} withdraw the designation, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} submit to the President an explanation or further
    explanation of why he designated the information as confidential,

  and where the person does neither of those things within the fifteen
  days, that information shall not thereafter be taken into account by
  the President in the proceedings for the purposes of which it was
  provided or in any proceedings arising out of those proceedings,
  unless the President obtains it elsewhere than from that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}President to reconsider

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, pursuant to subsection (1), a person submits
  to the President, within the fifteen days referred to in that
  subsection, an explanation or further explanation of why the person
  designated information as confidential, the President shall again
  consider whether, taking into account that explanation or further
  explanation, the designation of the information as confidential is
  warranted and, if the President decides that it is not warranted,
  shall cause the person to be notified that the information will not
  thereafter be taken into account by the President in the proceedings
  for the purposes of which it was provided or in any proceedings
  arising out of those proceedings, in which case the information shall
  not thereafter be taken into account by the President in any such
  proceedings, unless he obtains it elsewhere than from that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where failure to comply not rectified

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), where a person who has
  been informed pursuant to section 86 that he has failed to comply with
  paragraph 85(1)(b) with respect to any information does not, within
  fifteen days after being so informed or within such longer time not
  exceeding thirty days after being so informed as the President, either
  before or after the expiration of the fifteen days, in his discretion
  allows, take such action as it is necessary for the person to take in
  order to comply with paragraph 85(1)(b), the President shall cause the
  person to be notified that the information will not thereafter be
  taken into account by the President in the proceedings for the
  purposes of which it was provided or in any proceedings arising out of
  those proceedings, in which case the information shall not thereafter
  be taken into account by the President in any such proceedings, unless
  he obtains it elsewhere than from that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply in respect of any
  information that the President is prohibited by subsection (1) or (2)
  from taking into account in the proceedings for the purposes of which
  it was provided.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 87
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-87-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 87"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of sections 86 and 87

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** Sections 86 and 87 do not
apply in respect of evidence submitted to the President pursuant to
subsection 78(3).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. S-15, s. 88
- 1999, c. 17, s. 183
- 2005, c. 38, s. 134
:::
